8. Debt-funded revenue
9. DFRI estimates the share of covered companies' U.S. consumer revenue funded by new consumer credit. It also predicts each month's change in U.S. consumer borrowing before the Federal Reserve publishes the official G.19 figure, records that forecast with a timestamp, and grades it against the first print.

21. Tier definitions

82. Evidence Lift
83. Evidence Lift measures company-specific financing evidence, not risk, credit quality, or investment merit.

27. First revolving miss

29. One miss in one graded pair is statistically uninformative, but DFRI flags it rather than burying it. The model has not been retrained or retuned in response.

### User-visible information with native disclosures expanded

1. DFRI
2. CAMELON SYSTEMS
3. SCOREBOARD
4. COMPANIES
5. METHODOLOGY
6. CHANGELOG
7. DATA SCHEMA
8. Methodology
9. The nowcast estimates monthly G.19 credit flows. Attribution then applies registered product-to-category weights (Matrix A) and category-to-company weights (Matrix B), divided by estimated U.S. consumer revenue. Every judgmental uncertain input has a public assumption ID and band; fixed derived mappings retain their method and evidence.
10. Primary nowcast sources: Federal Reserve Board H.8 release archive, Census MARTS historic releases, and the Federal Reserve Board G.19 release archive used for first-print grading.
11. Read the complete code-derived modeling architecture, including implemented benchmarks, selection rules, point-in-time controls, attribution mechanics, and documented divergences.
12. Methodology 1.1.1 preserves the 50-company coverage boundary and corrects Carvana's auto-market and originated-finance classification. Compare 1.1.0 with 1.1.1 or inspect the dated coverage and exclusion ledger.
13. Bridge model
14. Partial H.8 months expose observed coverage explicitly. Missing retail data remains missing and is represented by an explicit availability indicator whose coefficient is fitted with the bridge.
15. First-print grading
16. The dated release page supplies the target-month preliminary level and revised prior-month level used for the release-coherent flow.
17. Ledger
18. Predictions and grades are separate. Model upgrades create new versions; old rows are neither restated nor deleted.
19. Tier 1
20. Observed: a disclosure directly links financing to the company's sales. The company page links the filing and a quote of no more than 15 words.
21. Tier 2
22. Category-mapped: credit flow is allocated to spend categories and then to companies by estimated category revenue share.
23. Tier 3
24. Fungible: unassignable flows are allocated in proportion to covered consumer revenue, with the widest bands.
25. Credit flow
26. The view uses the registered current-period midpoint flow, Matrix A midpoint, and Matrix B midpoint for each lane. It groups only after those published mappings are applied, keeps every remainder group within one evidence tier, and does not enter the nowcast or change any company estimate.
27. 2026-Q1 · registered midpoint allocations · millions of U.S. dollars
28. Width = estimated dollars
29. Style = how much is actually known

53. TIER 1 · OBSERVED LINK
54. TIER 2 · CATEGORY-MAPPED
55. TIER 3 · FUNGIBLE
56. The diagram begins with the portion of published G.19 flow represented by attribution lanes; it is not a complete use-of-funds decomposition. Tier 3 flows are proportional allocations, not observed transfers.
57. Exact static values behind the diagram. Highest-lift companies are shown separately; the remaining covered companies are bundled.
58. CREDIT LANE SPENDING CATEGORY TIER ESTIMATED MIDPOINT COVERED-COMPANY DESTINATIONS
59. Revolving + Nonrevolving Other Tier 1 TIER 1 $7,072M CVNA $540M; GM $2,970M; All other $3,562M
60. Revolving General retail TIER 2 $4,620M CVNA $36M; GM $199M; All other $4,385M
61. Revolving + Nonrevolving Tier 3 proportional TIER 3 $4,240M CVNA $33M; GM $183M; All other $4,024M
62. Nonrevolving Other Tier 2 TIER 2 $3,240M CVNA $221M; GM $1,232M; All other $1,787M
63. Evidence Lift
64. Evidence Lift equals a company's estimated DFR% midpoint divided by its pure Tier 3 fungibility baseline for the same period. The baseline is a counterfactual built from the already-computed broad proportional lanes—general retail plus the two fungible categories—with company-specific financing and auto-category evidence removed.
65. A value of 1.00× is baseline-only: no company-specific financing evidence was found, so the estimate reflects proportional allocation. A higher multiple shows how much registered evidence lifts the midpoint above that baseline. It does not measure risk, credit quality, or investment merit.
66. Backtest coverage
67. In the point-in-time backtest, the nominal 80% band contained 71.3% of first prints and the nominal 95% band contained 93.1%. Both currently under-cover their nominal rates, especially the 80% interval, so these bands are empirical uncertainty ranges, not guarantees.
68. Assumptions
69. Low / midpoint / high values are triangular priors unless all three values are identical. Source files are public so a cold clone can reproduce every result.
70. Every non-observed attribution parameter in methodology 1.1.1.
71. ID TIER STATEMENT LOW / MID / HIGH EVIDENCE SENSITIVITY NOTE
72. A-DEN-GM-001 TIER 2 GM U.S. consumer revenue is 45% to 65% of consolidated revenue. 0.45 / 0.55 / 0.65 Source
73. GM reports U.S. sales and GM Financial U.S. retail-sales penetration. A lower U.S.-consumer share raises GM's estimated DFR percentage.
74. A-DEN-F-001 TIER 2 Ford U.S. consumer revenue is 45% to 65% of consolidated revenue. 0.45 / 0.55 / 0.65 Source
75. Ford reports 2,204,124 U.S. vehicle sales for 2025. A lower U.S.-consumer share raises Ford's estimated DFR percentage.
76. A-DEN-AMZN-001 TIER 2 Amazon U.S. consumer revenue is 45% to 60% of consolidated revenue. 0.45 / 0.52 / 0.6 Source
77. North America, International, and AWS are separately disclosed reportable segments. The split between U.S. consumer activity and AWS/international revenue dominates the denominator.
78. A-DEN-WMT-001 TIER 2 Walmart U.S. consumer revenue is 68% to 76% of consolidated revenue. 0.68 / 0.72 / 0.76 Source
79. Walmart U.S., Walmart International, and Sam's Club U.S. are separately reported. The modeled U.S. share moves Walmart's denominator and aggregate index weight.
80. A-DEN-TGT-001 TIER 2 Target U.S. consumer revenue is 98% to 100% of consolidated revenue. 0.98 / 0.995 / 1 Source
81. Nearly all of our sales are in the U.S. Target's disclosed U.S. concentration makes this a narrow denominator prior.
82. A-DEN-LOW-001 TIER 2 Lowe's U.S. consumer revenue is 82% to 96% of consolidated revenue. 0.82 / 0.9 / 0.96 Source
83. Lowe's operated 1,759 stores and outlets in the United States. The range excludes modeled Canadian and professional-customer activity from consumer revenue.
84. A-DEN-HD-001 TIER 2 Home Depot U.S. consumer revenue is 75% to 90% of consolidated revenue. 0.75 / 0.82 / 0.9 Source
85. The filing separates U.S. retail activity from Canada, Mexico, and professional distribution. The consumer/professional split is the largest Home Depot denominator uncertainty.
86. A-DEN-BBY-001 TIER 2 Best Buy U.S. consumer revenue is 80% to 94% of consolidated revenue. 0.8 / 0.88 / 0.94 Source
87. Best Buy separately reports Domestic and International segment revenue. The prior removes international and modeled non-consumer revenue from the denominator.
88. A-DEN-ULTA-001 TIER 2 Ulta U.S. consumer revenue is 90% to 98% of consolidated revenue. 0.9 / 0.95 / 0.98 Source
89. Ulta has one reportable retail, salon, and e-commerce segment. The range allows for non-U.S. and non-consumer revenue without treating it as observed.
90. A-DEN-TSCO-001 TIER 2 Tractor Supply U.S. consumer revenue is 86% to 97% of consolidated revenue. 0.86 / 0.92 / 0.97 Source
91. The filing describes U.S. retail sales and tax-exempt agricultural customers. The range removes modeled business and tax-exempt agricultural sales from consumer revenue.
92. A-T1-GM-CAPTIVE-001 TIER 1 GM receives 8% to 14% of national nonrevolving flow through its captive-linked sales channel. 0.08 / 0.11 / 0.14 Source
93. GM Financial's penetration of our retail sales in the U.S. was 33%. This converts observed captive penetration into a share of national nonrevolving flow.
94. A-T1-F-CAPTIVE-001 TIER 1 Ford receives 7% to 14% of national nonrevolving flow through its captive-linked sales channel. 0.07 / 0.1 / 0.14 Source
95. The predominant share of Ford Credit's business consists of financing our vehicles. Current financing share is not disclosed; the wide prior is anchored to captive activity.
96. A-T1-AMZN-PAYMENTS-001 TIER 1 Amazon-linked branded cards and promotional financing receive 1% to 4% of revolving flow. 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.04 Source
97. We also offer co-branded credit card programs. Amazon does not disclose program purchase volume; this allocation remains wide.
98. A-T1-WMT-FINANCE-001 TIER 1 Walmart-linked cards and installment lending receive 0.4% to 1.5% of revolving flow. 0.004 / 0.008 / 0.015 Source
99. co-branded credit cards, limited access to cryptocurrency and equity investment products, installment lending The new program lacks a disclosed purchase-volume history, so the allocation is conservative.
100. A-T1-TGT-CARD-001 TIER 1 Target Circle cards receive 0.3% to 1.0% of national revolving flow. 0.003 / 0.006 / 0.01 Source
101. total Target Circle Card Penetration was 16.9 percent Observed store penetration is converted into an uncertain national-flow share.
102. A-T1-LOW-CARD-001 TIER 1 Lowe's branded cards receive 0.3% to 1.2% of national revolving flow. 0.003 / 0.006 / 0.012 Source
103. branded and private label proprietary credit cards which generate sales No purchase-volume percentage is disclosed; the program allocation is modeled.
104. A-T1-HD-CARD-001 TIER 1 Home Depot credit programs receive 0.4% to 1.5% of national revolving flow. 0.004 / 0.008 / 0.015 Source
105. third-party service providers who directly extend credit to customers Program volume is not disclosed and therefore remains a wide prior.
106. A-T1-BBY-CARD-001 TIER 1 Best Buy branded cards receive 0.2% to 1.0% of national revolving flow. 0.002 / 0.005 / 0.01 Source
107. We facilitate credit applications in our stores and online. The filing links the program to purchases but does not disclose purchase volume.
108. A-T1-ULTA-CARD-001 TIER 1 Ulta branded cards receive 0.05% to 0.4% of national revolving flow. 0.0005 / 0.0015 / 0.004 Source
109. Other revenue includes private label and co-branded credit card programs. Program volume is not disclosed; the range scales to Ulta's revenue footprint.
110. A-T1-TSCO-CARD-001 TIER 1 Tractor Supply private-label cards receive 0.05% to 0.4% of national revolving flow. 0.0005 / 0.0015 / 0.004 Source
111. Sales generated through the Company's private label credit cards Program volume is not disclosed; the range scales to Tractor Supply's footprint.
112. A-T2-REV-GENERAL-001 TIER 2 General retail receives 22% to 42% of national revolving-credit flow. 0.22 / 0.3 / 0.42 Source
113. MARTS publishes monthly retail sales by kind of business. This is the main Tier 2 category-mapping uncertainty for retailers.
114. A-T2-NONREV-AUTO-001 TIER 2 Covered auto sales receive 8% to 18% of national nonrevolving-credit flow. 0.08 / 0.12 / 0.18 Source
115. Household Debt and Credit reports auto-loan balances and originations. Nonrevolving credit includes education and other loans, so the auto share is uncertain.
116. A-T3-REV-FUNGIBLE-001 TIER 3 Six to fourteen percent of revolving flow is fungibly allocated to covered consumer revenue. 0.06 / 0.1 / 0.14 Source
117. BEA publishes monthly personal consumption expenditures by type. This widest-tier allocation tests unassignable revolving-credit use.
118. A-T3-NONREV-FUNGIBLE-001 TIER 3 Six to fourteen percent of nonrevolving flow is fungibly allocated to covered consumer revenue. 0.06 / 0.1 / 0.14 Source
119. BEA publishes monthly personal consumption expenditures by type. This widest-tier allocation captures personal and otherwise unassignable loans.
120. A-B-GENERAL-AMZN-001 TIER 2 Amazon receives 10% to 22% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.1 / 0.15 / 0.22 Source
121. Amazon reports North America retail sales and third-party seller services. Marketplace gross sales are not consolidated revenue, widening category share.
122. A-B-GENERAL-WMT-001 TIER 2 Walmart receives 15% to 28% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.15 / 0.21 / 0.28 Source
123. Walmart reports U.S. and Sam's Club U.S. net sales. Grocery and non-consumer components create the category-share range.
124. A-B-GENERAL-TGT-001 TIER 2 Target receives 4% to 9% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.04 / 0.06 / 0.09 Source
125. Target reports merchandise sales across six consumer categories. Food and household-essential mix changes its relevant general-retail share.
126. A-B-GENERAL-LOW-001 TIER 2 Lowe's receives 2% to 7% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.02 / 0.04 / 0.07 Source
127. Lowe's reports home-improvement retail net sales. Professional sales are removed through the denominator and category priors.
128. A-B-GENERAL-HD-001 TIER 2 Home Depot receives 3% to 8% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.03 / 0.05 / 0.08 Source
129. Home Depot reports retail and professional-customer net sales. Professional distribution makes the consumer category share uncertain.
130. A-B-GENERAL-BBY-001 TIER 2 Best Buy receives 1.5% to 4% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.015 / 0.025 / 0.04 Source
131. Best Buy reports Domestic consumer-electronics revenue. The range reflects overlap between electronics and general retail.
132. A-B-GENERAL-ULTA-001 TIER 2 Ulta receives 0.5% to 2% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.005 / 0.012 / 0.02 Source
133. Ulta reports retail, salon, and e-commerce revenue. Beauty is a narrow subset of general retail.
134. A-B-GENERAL-TSCO-001 TIER 2 Tractor Supply receives 0.5% to 2% of the covered general-retail allocation. 0.005 / 0.012 / 0.02 Source
135. Tractor Supply reports consumer and agricultural retail sales. Agricultural-business purchases broaden the category-share range.
136. A-B-AUTO-GM-001 TIER 2 GM receives 8% to 16% of the remaining covered auto allocation. 0.08 / 0.11 / 0.16 Source
137. GM reports U.S. vehicle sales and market share. Manufacturer revenue does not equal dealer retail receipts, so the share is banded.
138. A-B-AUTO-F-001 TIER 2 Ford receives 8% to 16% of the remaining covered auto allocation. 0.08 / 0.11 / 0.16 Source
139. Ford reports U.S. vehicle sales and market share. Manufacturer revenue does not equal dealer retail receipts, so the share is banded.
140. A-DEN-ABNB-001 TIER 2 Airbnb U.S. consumer revenue is 25% to 75% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 Source
141. The annual filing supplies consolidated platform revenue; U.S. guest activity and non-consumer usage are modeled conservatively. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
142. A-DEN-AZO-001 TIER 2 AutoZone U.S. consumer revenue is 65% to 94% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.65 / 0.8 / 0.94 Source
143. The annual filing supplies consolidated automotive-retail revenue; international and commercial-customer activity remain modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
144. A-DEN-BKNG-001 TIER 2 Booking Holdings U.S. consumer revenue is 12% to 55% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.12 / 0.3 / 0.55 Source
145. The annual filing supplies global travel-platform revenue; the U.S. consumer share is not directly reported and receives a wide band. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
146. A-DEN-CCL-001 TIER 2 Carnival Corporation U.S. consumer revenue is 30% to 78% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.3 / 0.55 / 0.78 Source
147. The annual filing supplies passenger-ticket and onboard revenue; passenger origin and non-U.S. activity require a modeled U.S. share. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
148. A-DEN-CVNA-001 TIER 2 Carvana U.S. consumer revenue is 78% to 99% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.78 / 0.9 / 0.99 Source
149. The annual filing supplies U.S. used-vehicle retail revenue; wholesale and ancillary components are conservatively removed. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
150. A-DEN-CASY-001 TIER 2 Casey's U.S. consumer revenue is 82% to 98% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.82 / 0.91 / 0.98 Source
151. The annual filing supplies U.S. convenience-retail revenue; fleet and other business purchases remain modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
152. A-DEN-CMG-001 TIER 2 Chipotle Mexican Grill U.S. consumer revenue is 86% to 99.5% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.86 / 0.94 / 0.995 Source
153. The annual filing supplies primarily U.S. restaurant revenue; the narrow band removes international and non-consumer sales. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
154. A-DEN-COST-001 TIER 2 Costco U.S. consumer revenue is 55% to 84% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.55 / 0.7 / 0.84 Source
155. The annual filing supplies geographic retail revenue; international and business-member purchases are modeled out. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
156. A-DEN-DRI-001 TIER 2 Darden Restaurants U.S. consumer revenue is 90% to 99.5% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.9 / 0.96 / 0.995 Source
157. The annual filing supplies predominantly U.S. restaurant revenue; a narrow band removes non-consumer activity. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
158. A-DEN-DECK-001 TIER 2 Deckers Brands U.S. consumer revenue is 25% to 72% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.25 / 0.48 / 0.72 Source
159. The annual filing supplies direct-to-consumer and wholesale brand revenue; U.S. consumer pass-through is modeled broadly. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
160. A-DEN-DG-001 TIER 2 Dollar General U.S. consumer revenue is 90% to 99.5% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.9 / 0.96 / 0.995 Source
161. The annual filing supplies U.S. retail revenue; the narrow band removes the limited non-consumer component. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
162. A-DEN-DLTR-001 TIER 2 Dollar Tree U.S. consumer revenue is 90% to 99.5% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.9 / 0.96 / 0.995 Source
163. The annual filing supplies U.S. and Canadian value-retail revenue; the band removes Canadian and non-consumer sales. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
164. A-DEN-DPZ-001 TIER 2 Domino's U.S. consumer revenue is 55% to 88% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.55 / 0.73 / 0.88 Source
165. The annual filing supplies U.S. stores, supply-chain, and international franchise revenue; consumer pass-through is modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
166. A-DEN-DASH-001 TIER 2 DoorDash U.S. consumer revenue is 55% to 90% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.55 / 0.75 / 0.9 Source
167. The annual filing supplies marketplace revenue; U.S. consumer orders are modeled separately from international and merchant services. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
168. A-DEN-EBAY-001 TIER 2 eBay U.S. consumer revenue is 30% to 75% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.3 / 0.52 / 0.75 Source
169. The annual filing supplies marketplace revenue; U.S. consumer transactions are modeled apart from international and business sellers. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
170. A-DEN-EXPE-001 TIER 2 Expedia Group U.S. consumer revenue is 28% to 72% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.28 / 0.5 / 0.72 Source
171. The annual filing supplies travel-platform revenue; geographic and business-travel activity receive a wide modeled split. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
172. A-DEN-HLT-001 TIER 2 Hilton Worldwide U.S. consumer revenue is 25% to 75% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75 Source
173. The annual filing supplies owned, managed, and franchise hotel revenue; U.S. leisure activity is modeled apart from business and international demand. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
174. A-DEN-KR-001 TIER 2 Kroger U.S. consumer revenue is 88% to 99.5% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.88 / 0.95 / 0.995 Source
175. The annual filing supplies predominantly U.S. food-retail revenue; a narrow band removes fuel, wholesale, and other non-consumer activity. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
176. A-DEN-LULU-001 TIER 2 Lululemon Athletica U.S. consumer revenue is 42% to 80% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.42 / 0.62 / 0.8 Source
177. The annual filing supplies direct consumer product revenue; geographic disclosures bound the modeled U.S. share. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
178. A-DEN-MAR-001 TIER 2 Marriott International U.S. consumer revenue is 24% to 72% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.24 / 0.48 / 0.72 Source
179. The annual filing supplies hotel management, franchise, and owned revenue; U.S. leisure demand is modeled apart from business and international demand. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
180. A-DEN-MCD-001 TIER 2 McDonald's U.S. consumer revenue is 30% to 70% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.7 Source
181. The annual filing supplies U.S. and international restaurant revenue; franchised-system consumer sales differ from reported revenue, widening the band. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
182. A-DEN-MGM-001 TIER 2 MGM Resorts U.S. consumer revenue is 55% to 88% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.55 / 0.72 / 0.88 Source
183. The annual filing supplies domestic and Macau resort revenue; U.S. leisure revenue is bounded with a broad consumer share. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
184. A-DEN-NKE-001 TIER 2 Nike U.S. consumer revenue is 28% to 72% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.28 / 0.5 / 0.72 Source
185. The annual filing supplies geographic and channel revenue; U.S. direct and wholesale consumer pass-through receives a wide band. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
186. A-DEN-NCLH-001 TIER 2 Norwegian Cruise Line Holdings U.S. consumer revenue is 35% to 80% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.35 / 0.58 / 0.8 Source
187. The annual filing supplies passenger-ticket and onboard revenue; U.S. passenger origin is modeled from global operations. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
188. A-DEN-ORLY-001 TIER 2 O'Reilly Automotive U.S. consumer revenue is 55% to 88% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.55 / 0.72 / 0.88 Source
189. The annual filing supplies U.S.-centric automotive retail revenue; professional-customer sales are removed through a broad band. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
190. A-DEN-RL-001 TIER 2 Ralph Lauren U.S. consumer revenue is 24% to 70% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.24 / 0.47 / 0.7 Source
191. The annual filing supplies geographic and channel revenue; U.S. direct and wholesale consumer pass-through is modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
192. A-DEN-ROST-001 TIER 2 Ross Stores U.S. consumer revenue is 93% to 99.9% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.93 / 0.975 / 0.999 Source
193. The annual filing supplies U.S. off-price retail revenue; only a minimal non-consumer share is modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
194. A-DEN-RCL-001 TIER 2 Royal Caribbean Group U.S. consumer revenue is 35% to 80% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.35 / 0.58 / 0.8 Source
195. The annual filing supplies passenger-ticket and onboard revenue; U.S. passenger origin is modeled from global operations. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
196. A-DEN-SBUX-001 TIER 2 Starbucks U.S. consumer revenue is 52% to 86% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.52 / 0.7 / 0.86 Source
197. The annual filing supplies North America, international, and channel-development revenue; U.S. consumer store revenue is modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
198. A-DEN-TSLA-001 TIER 2 Tesla U.S. consumer revenue is 25% to 70% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.25 / 0.47 / 0.7 Source
199. The annual filing supplies automotive, energy, and services revenue; U.S. consumer automotive revenue receives a wide modeled split. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
200. A-DEN-TJX-001 TIER 2 TJX Companies U.S. consumer revenue is 62% to 90% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.62 / 0.78 / 0.9 Source
201. The annual filing supplies U.S. and international off-price retail revenue; geographic disclosures bound the U.S. consumer share. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
202. A-DEN-WSM-001 TIER 2 Williams-Sonoma U.S. consumer revenue is 72% to 96% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.72 / 0.86 / 0.96 Source
203. The annual filing supplies U.S.-heavy direct retail revenue; business-to-business and international sales are modeled out. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
204. A-DEN-WYNN-001 TIER 2 Wynn Resorts U.S. consumer revenue is 20% to 58% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.2 / 0.38 / 0.58 Source
205. The annual filing supplies Las Vegas and Macau resort revenue; the U.S. consumer portion receives a wide geographic band. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
206. A-DEN-YUM-001 TIER 2 Yum! Brands U.S. consumer revenue is 22% to 64% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.22 / 0.42 / 0.64 Source
207. The annual filing supplies global franchise and company-restaurant revenue; U.S. consumer pass-through is modeled broadly. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
208. A-DEN-GRMN-001 TIER 2 Garmin U.S. consumer revenue is 25% to 72% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.25 / 0.48 / 0.72 Source
209. The annual filing supplies segment and geographic revenue; U.S. consumer products are modeled apart from aviation and commercial activity. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
210. A-DEN-HAS-001 TIER 2 Hasbro U.S. consumer revenue is 28% to 76% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.28 / 0.52 / 0.76 Source
211. The annual filing supplies consumer-products and entertainment revenue; U.S. consumer sell-through is modeled from consolidated revenue. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
212. A-DEN-EL-001 TIER 2 Estée Lauder U.S. consumer revenue is 18% to 62% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.18 / 0.38 / 0.62 Source
213. The annual filing supplies geographic and product-category revenue; U.S. direct and wholesale consumer pass-through receives a wide band. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
214. A-DEN-PEP-001 TIER 2 PepsiCo U.S. consumer revenue is 35% to 74% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.35 / 0.55 / 0.74 Source
215. The annual filing supplies geographic food-and-beverage revenue; U.S. consumer sell-through is modeled from manufacturer revenue. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
216. A-DEN-PG-001 TIER 2 Procter & Gamble U.S. consumer revenue is 22% to 64% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.22 / 0.42 / 0.64 Source
217. The annual filing supplies geographic consumer-products revenue; U.S. consumer sell-through is modeled from manufacturer revenue. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
218. A-DEN-TPR-001 TIER 2 Tapestry U.S. consumer revenue is 25% to 72% of consolidated annual revenue. 0.25 / 0.48 / 0.72 Source
219. The annual filing supplies geographic and direct-to-consumer channel revenue; U.S. consumer revenue remains modeled. This modeled geographic and customer-mix split moves the company denominator and its revenue weight in the aggregate index.
220. A-T1-CVNA-FINANCE-001 TIER 1 Carvana receives 1% to 3% of national nonrevolving flow through its originated used-auto finance channel. 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.03 Source
221. Since February 2013, Carvana has also offered and originated loans to consumers. The wide prior is scaled below GM and Ford using Carvana's smaller registered consumer-revenue denominator; the filing and ABS trust establish origination.
222. Matrix A and B
223. Matrix A has 15 product-to-category rows. Matrix B has 165 category-to-company rows. Tests reject negative weights, missing evidence, unregistered uncertainty, or row allocations over 100%.

9. The P1 universe partitions every Consumer Discretionary and Consumer Staples member in the pinned, cross-checked S&P 500 snapshot. Historical coverage is preserved by methodology version; no past company set is rewritten.
10. Verified 2026-08-05. Fifty included, 31 excluded, with a one-line reason for every exclusion. Retain the ten P0 issuers, then rank passing issuers by direct consumer-revenue evidence, geographic evidence, filing completeness, and ticker as the deterministic tie-break. No price, market-cap, or vendor data is used.

10. 2026-08-08 · publication · 1.1.1
11. Numbers promoted; credit flow added
12. Compressed the heading scale so figures carry the hierarchy and added a server-rendered current-period flow view whose ribbon width represents estimated dollars and whose solid, dashed, and dotted styles expose evidence tiers without color dependence.
13. Credit flow · Methodology
14. 2026-08-08 · publication · bridge-ridge-v2-alpha10
15. First live grades published without softening
16. Published the first two live grades, prominently flagging that the revolving prediction missed direction and its 80% interval; added running live-only coverage and MAE comparisons without retraining or retuning the model.
17. Scoreboard · Scoreboard feed
18. 2026-08-08 · publication · 1.1.1
19. Graded feed status corrected
20. Corrected the scoreboard compatibility status so every graded row reports GRADED, matching grade\_status; the feed schema now states and tests that invariant.
21. Scoreboard feed · Feed schema
22. 2026-08-06 · methodology · 1.1.1
23. Carvana auto-finance classification corrected
24. Added Carvana's SEC-filed originated-auto-finance and ABS trust evidence, included CVNA in the covered-auto allocation, and published the derived Evidence Lift ranking under a non-breaking v2 company-feed schema.
25. Evidence Lift · Carvana · Company feed v2 · Feed schema v2
26. 2026-08-05 · publication · 1.1.0
27. Quarterly attribution refresh demonstrated
28. Recomputed the 2026-Q1 50-company attribution from six Board first-print monthly flows and point-in-time SEC filing facts; 35 denominators advanced through comparable 10-Q facts and 15 remained explicitly on their annual baseline.
29. Quarterly refresh feed · Company history feed · Methodology
30. 2026-08-05 · methodology · 1.1.0
31. P1 coverage expanded to 50 companies
32. Expanded the evidence-first consumer-company universe from 10 to 50, published the dated 31-company exclusion ledger, and retained methodology 1.0.0 results for direct comparison.
33. Coverage ledger · Version comparison · Company feed
34. 2026-08-05 · publication · 1.0.0

37. Scoreboard · Methodology
38. 2026-08-05 · publication · 1.0.0
